=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool :
        indexes = collections.defaultdict(collections.deque)
        for index, character in enumerate(s) :
            indexes[int(character)].append(index)

        unique_t_values = set([int(t_i) for t_i in t])
        for t_v in unique_t_values : 
            if t_v not in indexes :
                return False 
            
        for character in t : 
            character_designation = int(character)
            if character_designation not in indexes : 
                return False 
            for lesser_character in range(character_designation):
                if indexes[lesser_character] and indexes[lesser_character][0] < indexes[character_designation][0] : 
                    return False 
            indexes[character_designation].popleft()
            if len(indexes[character_designation]) == 0 : 
                indexes.pop(character_designation)
        
        # if all characters able to sort, return it 
        return False

Let's recall known LeetCode 1585 solution:
```
class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        pos = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            pos[int(ch)].append(i)
        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not pos[d]:
                return False
            for smaller in range(d):
                if pos[smaller] and pos[smaller][0] < pos[d][0]:
                    return False
            pos[d].popleft()
        return True
```

from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        indexes = defaultdict(deque)
        for index, character in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(character)].append(index)

        for character in t:
            d = int(character)

                return False

                if indexes[lesser] and indexes[lesser][0] < indexes[d][0]:
                    return False
            indexes[d].popleft()
            if not indexes[d]:

        return True

```
def isTransformable(self, s, t):
    idx = defaultdict(deque)
    for i, ch in enumerate(s):
        idx[int(ch)].append(i)
    for ch in t:
        d = int(ch)
        if not idx[d]: return False
        for i in range(d):
            if idx[i] and idx[i][0] < idx[d][0]:
                return False
        idx[d].popleft()
    return True
```

```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        positions = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            positions[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not positions[d]:
                return False
            for smaller in range(d):
                if positions[smaller] and positions[smaller][0] < positions[d][0]:
                    return False
            positions[d].popleft()
            if not positions[d]:
                del positions[d]

        return True
```

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool :
        indexes = collections.defaultdict(collections.deque)
        for index, character in enumerate(s) :
            indexes[int(character)].append(index)

        unique_t_values = set([int(t_i) for t_i in t])
        for t_v in unique_t_values : 
            if t_v not in indexes :
                return False

            character_designation = int(character)
            if character_designation not in indexes : 
                return False 
            for lesser_character in range(character_designation):
                if indexes[lesser_character] and indexes[lesser_character][0] < indexes[character_designation][0] : 
                    return False 
            indexes[character_designation].popleft()

                indexes.pop(character_designation)
        
        # if all characters able to sort, return it 
        return False

Need maybe produce final code with minimal changes:
```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        indexes = defaultdict(deque)
        for index, character in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(character)].append(index)

        for character in t:
            d = int(character)
            if not indexes[d]:
                return False
            for lesser in range(d):
                if indexes[lesser] and indexes[lesser][0] < indexes[d][0]:
                    return False
            indexes[d].popleft()
            if not indexes[d]:
                del indexes[d]

        return True
```
Need decide if `if not indexes[d]` might create key d and then `for lesser` uses `indexes[d][0]`? We only continue if not empty. Good.

Need maybe include `from collections import defaultdict, deque` and class. If no code fence, it's still code. I'll output:
```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        indexes = defaultdict(deque)
        for index, character in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(character)].append(index)

        for character in t:
            d = int(character)
            if not indexes[d]:
                return False
            for lesser in range(d):
                if indexes[lesser] and indexes[lesser][0] < indexes[d][0]:
                    return False
            indexes[d].popleft()
            if not indexes[d]:
                del indexes[d]

        return True
```

def isTransformable(self, s, t):
    n = len(s)
    pos = [[] for _ in range(10)]
    for i, ch in enumerate(s):
        pos[int(ch)].append(i)
    for ch in t:
        d = int(ch)
        if not pos[d]:
            return False
        for i in range(d):
            if pos[i] and pos[i][0] < pos[d][0]:
                return False

    return True

```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        pos = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            pos[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not pos[d]:
                return False
            for smaller in range(d):
                if pos[smaller] and pos[smaller][0] < pos[d][0]:
                    return False
            pos[d].popleft()
            if not pos[d]:
                del pos[d]
        return True
```

```
import collections

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        indexes = collections.defaultdict(collections.deque)
        for index, character in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(character)].append(index)

        for character in t:
            d = int(character)
            if not indexes[d]:
                return False
            for lesser in range(d):
                if indexes[lesser] and indexes[lesser][0] < indexes[d][0]:
                    return False
            indexes[d].popleft()
            if not indexes[d]:
                del indexes[d]
        return True
```
This is close to original. But note if d absent, `if not indexes[d]` creates empty key. Fine. If d absent and we return false. Good. If d present empty, return false. Good. This code uses `collections` module. It doesn't have unique_t_values check. If we want to keep unique check, we could:
```

...

        for v in unique_t_values:
            if v not in indexes:
                return False
...
```
But not necessary. The original had unique check; removing it is okay. But maybe if we remove, we need ensure `if not indexes[d]` handles absent. It does. Good.

Now, let's consider if the algorithm should process t from right to left instead. I want to be absolutely sure. Let's revisit known solution. I recall a LeetCode problem "Check If String Is Transformable With Substring Sort Operations" solution uses a greedy from left to right with positions and checking smaller digits. Let me search memory: There is a solution:
```
def isTransformable(self, s, t):
    n = len(s)
    pos = [[] for _ in range(10)]
    for i, ch in enumerate(s):
        pos[int(ch)].append(i)
    for ch in t:
        d = int(ch)
        if not pos[d]:
            return False
        for i in range(d):
            if pos[i] and pos[i][0] < pos[d][0]:
                return False
        pos[d].pop(0)
    return True
```
Yes, left-to-right. Good.

Or maybe condition is different: For each digit in t, check if there is a smaller digit after it? Let's investigate.

This is crucial. Let's verify LeetCode 1585 algorithm. I might be misremembering. Let's reason from first principles.

Operation: sort any substring ascending. This operation can move smaller digits left and larger digits right. It cannot move a larger digit left past a smaller digit. Therefore, for any pair of digits a < b, the relative order of a and b in the final string t must be consistent with some constraints? Actually sorting ascending can change relative order of a and b only by moving a left of b if originally b before a. It can never move b left of a if originally a before b. So for each pair a < b, if in s a appears before b, then in t a must appear before b? Wait if a before b in s, sorting ascending preserves a before b? Let's test s=2,1 (a=1,b=2). In s, a=1 is after b=2, so a before b false. Sorting whole string gives 1,2, so a before b true. So relative order can change from b before a to a before b, but not from a before b to b before a. Thus for a<b, if a is before b in s, then a must be before b in t. If b is before a in s, t can have either order (because sorting can move a left of b). This is a partial order constraint.

Now, algorithm left-to-right checking smaller before current? Let's derive.

For each digit d in t, we choose an occurrence of d in s. If there is a smaller digit x < d whose chosen occurrence is before the chosen d occurrence in s, then in t, x should appear before d? Actually if x before d in s, then x must remain before d in t (because x<d and x before d in s cannot be reversed). Therefore d cannot be placed at current position if an unused x before it will need to be before d. So condition: no smaller digit's earliest unused occurrence before d's chosen occurrence. This is what algorithm does. For t=2143, let's apply with original positions and see if it should return false.

The constraint is if smaller x before larger d in s, then x must remain before d. Here 3 is after 4, so no constraint; 4 before 3 is allowed but not guaranteed. So algorithm says okay. Since 3 was after 4 in s, 4 before 3 is the original order; sorting can move 3 left of 4, but can it move 4 back left of 3? No, because 4>3. Once 3 moves left of 4, cannot reverse. Let's decide final code:
```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        indexes = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not indexes[d]:
                return False
            for smaller in range(d):
                if indexes[smaller] and indexes[smaller][0] < indexes[d][0]:
                    return False
            indexes[d].popleft()
            if not indexes[d]:
                del indexes[d]

        return True
```

```
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        positions = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            positions[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not positions[d]:
                return False

            for smaller in range(d):
                if positions[smaller] and positions[smaller][0] < positions[d][0]:
                    return False

            positions[d].popleft()
            if not positions[d]:
                del positions[d]

        return True
```
This is good.

Let's maybe think about proof of algorithm to be confident. The operation sorts a substring ascending. This is equivalent to repeatedly swapping adjacent inversions (larger before smaller) within the substring. Thus any transform is a sequence of swaps of adjacent pairs (x,y) where x>y, swapping to y,x. This means a smaller digit can move left past a larger digit, but a larger digit cannot move left past a smaller digit. Therefore, for each digit d, when it is placed in t at position i, it must be that among the remaining digits smaller than d, none are before its original position, because they would have to move right past d? Actually if smaller x is before d in current string, then x<d, and to place d before x, we would need to swap d left past x, which is a larger-left move, impossible. So d can only move left past larger digits. Thus the condition.

The greedy algorithm processes t left-to-right. For current target digit d, choose the earliest remaining occurrence of d in s. If any smaller digit x has its earliest remaining occurrence before this d, then in the current string x is before d, and since x<d, d cannot be moved before x. So impossible. If no such x, then d can be moved to the front of the remaining string by sorting the prefix from the start of remaining string to d's position? Need ensure. The current remaining string (after removing already placed digits) has d's chosen occurrence, and all digits before it are larger than d (because no smaller before it). Sorting the substring from the start of remaining string to d's position will move d to the front (since d is the smallest in that prefix). This simulates placing d. Then remove d and continue. This is constructive. Good.

Need maybe think if "remaining string" after removing already placed digits corresponds to original positions with some removed. The algorithm uses original positions; if no smaller before d's original position among remaining, then in the current string, all remaining digits before d are larger. Sorting prefix can move d to front. Good.

Need maybe think if there is any issue with digits equal to d before d? Equal digits indistinguishable; choosing earliest occurrence. If another d before chosen d, it is also d, no blocker. Sorting prefix with equal d will place one d at front; choosing earliest is fine. Good.

Need maybe think if there is any issue with `indexes[lesser]` comparing earliest unused smaller to earliest unused d. If there is a smaller digit before d but it's not the earliest unused smaller? Earliest is before any other, so if any before, earliest before. Good.

Need maybe think if there is any issue with `indexes[lesser]` being empty but there is a used smaller before d? Used smaller already placed in t, not in remaining string, so doesn't block. Good.

Need maybe think if there is any issue with `indexes[d]` being empty but there is a used d before? Used d already placed, no remaining d. If t still needs d, impossible. Good.

Need maybe think if there is any issue with `indexes[d]` being empty but there is a used d before? Used d already placed, no remaining d. If t still needs d, impossible. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        indexes = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not indexes[d]:
                return False

            for smaller in range(d):
                if indexes[smaller] and indexes[smaller][0] < indexes[d][0]:
                    return False

            indexes[d].popleft()
            if not indexes[d]:
                del indexes[d]

        return True
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from collections import defaultdict, deque

class Solution:
    def isTransformable(self, s: str, t: str) -> bool:
        if len(s) != len(t):
            return False

        indexes = defaultdict(deque)
        for i, ch in enumerate(s):
            indexes[int(ch)].append(i)

        for ch in t:
            d = int(ch)
            if not indexes[d]:
                return False

            for smaller in range(d):
                if indexes[smaller] and indexes[smaller][0] < indexes[d][0]:
                    return False

            indexes[d].popleft()
            if not indexes[d]:
                del indexes[d]

        return True
